# Campaign of 2026-10-05, campaign tier, mcp arm, discovery

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:81d5f62acb289022175d192df0a61faf5f71eb4b79e8dd1f4efae4532ebdd916
lock: sha256:b768fe795c8d44a882e8604a5012c2eb5f2b4d584e7445367dfb24bb21c3585d
selection: sha256:c84af3cc500bdabda0be3c6dd5e76b38223d5e9950b0fc012f9fc68e7c9a1e32
read_set: sha256:0934c679a536b4e44258201b27ca9ee9bea11fa827b636e241fed118610860c7
seed: 0
harness: 0.5.0
tier: campaign
arm: mcp
at: 2026-10-05
cost_cents: 10556
```

**This line is one of the paid layer campaign that [#1659](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1659) asked for.** `tools/probe/campaign.sh` recorded it as line 6 of the plan of batch B, a plan that draws its lines from `tools/probe/layer-campaign.spec`. It is the mcp arm of the campaign tier over the discovery selection, at 118 repetitions per probe, on `claude-sonnet-5` under Claude Code 2.1.288 with a cap of 80 turns for each session. The line leaves out `HW-PROBE-the-authoring-skill-reaches-an-agent-that-is-about-to-write-a-governed-document`, because no arm without `.claude/` can satisfy it.

**One tree.** Every session ran in a fresh copy of a `git archive` of commit `debe0c70`, with the engine of that commit. Batch B ran after batch A on the same pin that batch A ended on.

**What the recorder counted.** The line holds 354 sessions and cost 10556 cents. The intent hook was live in 354 of them. None stopped at the turn cap, and none at the session budget. The recorder reports 11 paths outside the workspace named in a call of this line, 0 sessions uncounted, and 0 web-tool calls.

**The contract of this batch.** The sessions ran under the recorder contract of spec 15: a `bwrap` sandbox with no network of its own, an empty configuration directory, `--setting-sources project,local`, the permission mode `dontAsk`, and `WebSearch` and `WebFetch` denied. The batches of 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-30 ran under `bypassPermissions` and the configuration of the host, so a rate from this batch does not compare with a rate from those batches.

**Where the figures are.** The rates, the intervals and the comparisons of this line are in the generated result that reads this transcript, and this document states none of them.

A rule is advisory when the remedy is a rewrite.\\\" Find where severity is actually encoded (enum in Rust code and/or field in the taxonomy declaration).\\n\\n4. Find a handful of real rule IDs and their severities from the taxonomy/overlay files.\\n\\nReport file paths with line numbers for everything so I can go edit precisely. I need to find:\\n\\n1. In `engine/crates/check/`: how existing rules are structured/implemented — show me one or two example rule files (ideally one that checks a front-matter/metadata field, like checking for an empty or placeholder field, e.g. anything related to `unwritten_section`, `summary`, or similar). Show the full source of 1-2 representative rule files, including how a rule registers its id, its severity (error vs advisory), and how it emits a finding/diagnostic.\\n2. Where severities (error/advisory) are declared — is severity declared in the taxonomy (`.headwater/overlay.yml`, `taxonomy-source/*/taxonomy.yml`, or a bundle) or hardcoded in the Rust rule implementation? Show the exact taxonomy YAML shape for declaring a rule (fields like id, severity, description, applies_to, etc.) with a concrete existing example copied verbatim.\\n3. Where rule fixtures live (both the \\\"failing fixture\\\" test input and the \\\"discriminating condition\\\" test) — show the directory structure and one example fixture + its corresponding Rust test, for a rule similar in spirit to \\\"field X is empty\\\".\\n4. Confirm whether `summary` is an existing front-matter field already declared in the taxonomy (search for `summary:` in docs front matter and in taxonomy YAML files) — I want to know if `summary` is already a recognized facet/field name, and whether there's already partial validation of it (e.g. required-ness) elsewhere.\\n\\nReport file paths and line numbers, and quote the relevant snippets verbatim (don't just describe them) so I can write the new rule by example. Keep the report focused — under 600 words of prose plus the quoted snippets.\"}"

I need to add a new check rule: \\\"report a document whose summary field is empty.\\\" I need to understand the existing pattern for how rules are declared and implemented, so I can add a consistent one.\\n\\nPlease investigate and report back:\\n\\n1. In `taxonomy-source/` (and possibly `.headwater/overlay.yml` or `.headwater/taxonomy.yml`), find where checks/rules are declared — e.g. look for existing rules similar to \\\"field is empty\\\" or \\\"required facet missing\\\" or rules about the `summary` facet specifically. A rule is advisory when the remedy is a rewrite\\\").\\n\\n2. Find the severity vocabulary used (e.g. \\\"error\\\" | \\\"advisory\\\") and where rule declarations list `remedy` type (mechanical vs rewrite), since CLAUDE.md states the severity depends on whether the remedy is mechanical-and-total or a rewrite.\\n\\nReport file paths, line numbers, and relevant code/YAML excerpts.     - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Locate rule declaration and check implementation pattern\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"I'm working in the Headwater repository (a documentation-governance engine). I need to add a new rule to the engine that reports a document whose `summary` field is empty. Before writing anything, I need to understand the existing pattern for how a \\\"rule\\\" is declared and implemented in this codebase, so I can follow it exactly.\\n\\nPlease investigate and report back with concrete file paths and line numbers:\\n\\n1. Where are rules/checks DECLARED in the taxonomy? Look at `.headwater/taxonomy.yml`, `.headwater/overlay.yml`, `.headwater/packages/`, `taxonomy-source/headwater-standard/`, and `docs/taxonomies/`. Find how an existing rule (e.g. something checking a required field is non-empty, or a British-spelling/contraction/hard-wrap rule mentioned in CLAUDE.md) is declared — what keys/structure does a rule declaration use (id, severity, message, category, etc.)? Is severity (error vs advisory) declared there or elsewhere?\\n\\n2. Where are rules IMPLEMENTED in the engine source code? Look under `engine/crates/`. Find the crate/module that implements checks (likely something like a \\\"lint\\\" or \\\"rules\\\" or \\\"checks\\\" module). Find an existing rule implementation that is structurally similar to \\\"a required string field is empty\\\" — e.g. a check on `summary`, `title`, or another required front-matter facet being empty or missing. Give the exact file path and function/struct name.\\n\\n3. Is there an existing facet/field in the taxonomy called `summary` on documents? Where is it declared as a required facet? (Check `.headwater/taxonomy.lock`, `.headwater/taxonomy.yml`, or docs/taxonomies/*.)\\n\\n4. How does CLAUDE.md's rule \\\"A rule is an error when its remedy is mechanical and total... A rule is advisory when the remedy is a rewrite\\\" map to actual severity values in the rule declaration schema (e.g. is there a literal `severity: error` / `severity: advisory` enum)? Find one example of each.\\n\\n5. Is there a test fixture pattern for rules (e.g. `engine/crates/*/tests/` or fixture docs) that I'd need to mirror for a new rule?\\n\\nReport file paths, line numbers, and short code/YAML excerpts for each of these 5 points. Keep the report focused and under 600 words — I need concrete locations, not general summary.\"}"
      result: ""

  session: "L6-campaign-mcp-p2-r103"
  calls:
    - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find doc ruling on merged shared totals\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"In this repository (a documentation/governance system called Headwater, under /var/tmp/hw-1659/b/ws/L6-campaign-mcp-p2-r103), I'm looking for a specific governing document that addresses this exact scenario:\\n\\nTwo branches each add one page/document to the corpus. Each branch also independently updates a \\\"total\\\" value in some shared file (e.g., a count of documents, a manifest, an index with a count field). Git merges the two branches cleanly with NO textual conflict (e.g., because the two edits touch different lines, or because it's a simple increment in the same spot that doesn't look like a conflict), but the resulting total on the merged branch is now WRONG (stale/incorrect) because git's merge doesn't know that the field is a derived/computed value that both sides changed independently.\\n\\nThis is the general class of problem known as \\\"derived artifact\\\" or \\\"fold\\\" merge correctness — i.e. a value that is derived from other content and must be regenerated/recomputed rather than naively text-merged.\\n\\nThe CLAUDE.md at the repo root mentions:\\n- `.gitattributes` commits `-merge` for each \\\"derived fold\\\"\\n- `.githooks/merge-regenerate` as a merge driver that regenerates derived artifacts\\n- `merge.headwater-regenerate.driver` git config\\n- HW-DR-0054 (docs/decisions/0054-the-upper-bound-of-a-reconcile-first-allocator-is-the-corpus-and-a-claim-store.md) is about identifier claim stores, likely NOT this issue (that's about allocator upper bounds / claim files, a related but distinct problem)\\n\\nI need you to find the actual document (likely under docs/decisions/, docs/requirements/, docs/subsystems/, docs/spec/, or docs/process/decisions/) that specifically RULES on how a derived total / count / fold kept in a shared file must be maintained across merges — i.e. the rule that such values must not be hand-maintained/git-merged as text but instead regenerated, or the rule defining what a \\\"derived fold\\\" is and why.\\n\\nSearch for terms like: \\\"derived fold\\\", \\\"regenerate\\\", \\\"merge driver\\\", \\\"fold\\\", \\\"total\\\", \\\"count\\\", \\\"derived artifact\\\", \\\"merge-regenerate\\\", \\\"commutative\\\", \\\"idempotent regeneration\\\" within docs/decisions/, docs/requirements/, docs/subsystems/, docs/process/decisions/, docs/spec/.\\n\\nReport back: the exact file path of the single best-matching document, its title, and the key sentence(s) that make it the ruling document for this scenario. If there are multiple candidates, list them ranked, with the best one first and why it's the best match over HW-DR-0054.\"}"
      result: ""

      result: ""

  answer: null

    - tool: "Skill"
      argument: "{\"skill\":\"headwater-orient\",\"args\":\"which shelf a new document belongs on, and where its identifier comes from — how headwater new decides the shelf and mints or claims the identifier\"}"
      result: ""

      argument: "{\"format\":\"text\"}"
      result: ""

      argument: "{\"skill\":\"headwater-authoring\",\"args\":\"how does headwater new decide which shelf a new document belongs on, and where does the document's identifier come from\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find identifier scheme assignment rules\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"In this repository (Headwater), I need to understand how a document's identifier is determined/minted when it's created via `headwater new`. I already know from docs:\\n- Each kind declares a shelf (file path pattern), sometimes with a \\\"discriminator\\\" facet like doc_type for spec_series or reviews shelves.\\n- The identifier claim store: `headwater new` claims each identifier it mints as one file under `.headwater/ids/<scheme>/<identifier>`, written once (cites HW-DR-0054, \\\"the upper bound of a reconcile-first allocator is the corpus and a claim store\\\").\\n- obligation doc 0107 says `kinds.specification` names no identifier scheme, so `headwater new specification` refuses with \\\"Unnameable\\\".\\n\\nI need to find: where in docs/spec/ or docs/decisions/ or docs/process/decisions/ the identifier scheme mechanism is specified — i.e., how a kind is bound to an identifier scheme (e.g. sequential numeric prefix like 0054-, or something else), how the scheme decides the next number (reconcile against corpus + claim store), and whether different shelves/kinds use different numbering sequences (e.g., does docs/decisions/ share a counter with docs/process/decisions/, or are they independent?). Also check docs/process/decisions/0024-a-document-that-moves-to-a-process-shelf-keeps-its-identifier.md for relevant detail about identifier schemes continuing across shelves.\\n\\nPlease read:\\n- docs/decisions/0054-the-upper-bound-of-a-reconcile-first-allocator-is-the-corpus-and-a-claim-store.md\\n- docs/obligations/0107-the-base-package-ships-a-kind-that-the-scaffolder-refuses-to-write.md\\n- docs/process/decisions/0024-a-document-that-moves-to-a-process-shelf-keeps-its-identifier.md\\n- any docs/spec/*.md file whose title/section concerns identifier schemes (search for \\\"identifier scheme\\\" or \\\"scheme\\\" in docs/spec and docs/decisions)\\n\\nReport back (under 300 words): how identifier schemes are declared/bound to kinds, how the actual next identifier number is computed (reconcile-first allocator across corpus + claim store + remote branches), and whether shelf and identifier scheme are coupled or independent axes (i.e. can two kinds on different shelves share one numbering scheme, or does each shelf get its own sequence). Cite file paths and identifiers (HW-DR-xxxx, etc).\"}"
      result: ""
